Guild: comma.ai community
Channel: dev_topics / fw-mods
Topic: firmware mods can be extremely dangerous! no walkthroughs, no copyrighted links, just discussion about how to be safe. the existence of this channel is not an endorsement of modifying your firmware. substantially over 2x torque mods will get you banned from our servers starting 3/22
==============================================================

Seems people don’t understand that they are only overcoming the torque because the steer sensor is detecting their input and then counteracting the EPS for them. You aren’t actually overcoming anything. Is that an accurate way to describe it? 

Which would explain how it could easily create a situation where you are no longer in control.


[1/8/2020 2:35 PM] georgehotz

So I agree that 2x is good

[1/9/2020 9:41 PM] gernby
Has anyone (including Comma) tried a non-linear adjustment to the torque tables to achieve a more linear response from the Honda EPS?  My experience has been that my 2018 Accord has really great response and resolution near center, but the the response drops VERY quickly as it departs from center.  Basically, the top half of the torque table is virtually useless.  If I had access to my torque tables, I would want to leave it alone near the center, and apply a parabolic factor to the outer range.


[1/9/2020 9:45 PM] bugsyborromeo
that's assuming the stock table is linear?


[1/9/2020 9:49 PM] gernby
I don't know anything about the stock table, but the results from it are definitely NOT linear.  My suggestion is to adjust the table so that the results are more linear


[1/9/2020 9:49 PM] gernby
Accomplishing that would require some smart datalogging


[1/9/2020 9:50 PM] bugsyborromeo
yeah not linear
joe sent a screenshot somewhere with the stock one


[1/9/2020 9:51 PM] gernby
My assumption is and was that the table isn't linear, since the behavior isn't linear.  That's why I'm suggesting a non-linear adjustment


[1/9/2020 9:52 PM] jeff12345515253
@gernby joe has tried a linear torque table with some success


[1/9/2020 9:52 PM] jeff12345515253
stock is not linear


[1/9/2020 9:53 PM] gernby
I agree


[1/9/2020 9:54 PM] gernby
Stock is not linear, and Joe has had "some" success from linear adjustments


[1/9/2020 9:54 PM] gernby
If I haven't been clear, I am suggesting a NON-linear adjustment to make the torque MORE linear

[1/10/2020 10:07 AM] gernby
Feedforward is basically the "best guess" for a torque value based entirely on speed and desired angle


[1/10/2020 10:07 AM] gernby
The default guess is very low


[1/10/2020 10:08 AM] fleetdog1
Right but currently its .00006 so for example doubling that means what


[1/10/2020 10:08 AM] gernby
In my fork, Kf is dynamic


[1/10/2020 10:08 AM] gernby
Doubling it would double the guess


[1/10/2020 10:08 AM] fleetdog1
Does that change the time factor for how early a prediction is acted on or amplify the reaction or what


[1/10/2020 10:08 AM] bugsyborromeo
time factor is done with actuatordelay


[1/10/2020 10:09 AM] gernby
No.  It just changes the conversion from angle to torque


[1/10/2020 10:09 AM] bugsyborromeo
basically at highway speeds and tighter curves, it'll command more torque with a higher value


[1/10/2020 10:10 AM] fleetdog1
Interesting but doesnt the mpc already do this I mean I see turn in before error hits all the time like its trying to hit the apex


[1/10/2020 10:10 AM] vaggysag
What can I do to tune out swaying from model uncertainty on a straight?


[1/10/2020 10:10 AM] fleetdog1
Or is this the factor that changes what the mpc does


[1/10/2020 10:10 AM] gernby
The torque request is a combination of feedforward + proportional error + integral error.  In my fork, I maximize the use of feedforward using a rate based value near center, and angle based value (dynamic) outside of center.  That results in much less reliance on steering error to steer the car.

[1/16/2020 6:17 AM] .nikonian
@FleetDog @wocsor looking at pins for pd70f4155 and ridgeline board, I see 17-23 have direct traces to debug pads. Others cross back and forth from back side of board to front (may even be multilayer board) I am hesitant to use continuity probing to trace these as I don't want to change any states if this chip has persistent memory. Of course I can use machinist mirror or bright back light to identify.

[1/16/2020 10:13 AM] crumresearch
@Martin : See this pull request for 0.7.1 code changes, including changes like this to  `selfdrive/car/honda/carcontroller.py` :
https://github.com/commaai/openpilot/commit/8da8b6135c600ec653c16606239364ab9324fd98#diff-a4e5443b647b590ccada379c91efcbb8R87-R91
```
    self.eps_modified = False
    for fw in CP.carFw:
      if fw.ecu == "eps" and b"," in fw.fwVersion:
        print("EPS FW MODIFIED!")
        self.eps_modified = True
...

    if CS.CP.carFingerprint in (CAR.CIVIC) and self.eps_modified:
      if apply_steer > 0xA00:
        apply_steer = (apply_steer - 0xA00) / 2 + 0xA00
      elif apply_steer < -0xA00:
        apply_steer = (apply_steer + 0xA00) / 2 - 0xA00

[1/17/2020 9:04 AM] fleetdog1
Yeah lets see what each one goes to


[1/17/2020 10:03 AM] .nikonian
There are 2  tx and 2 rx pins, (cno and cn1). I expect cno would be 1st com port. These go out to test points not debug pad. Im pulling out all 4 of these connections. The others do appear on debug pads. vss and vdd appear to be same as evss and evdd in that they are tied together. One thing that is unexpected is MD0 and VSS are absolutely tied together, and appear at outermost debug pad. MD0 and VDD are isolated and have their own pads.


[1/17/2020 10:04 AM] .nikonian
In my CNC machine experiance md0 is a "drive" that contains machine software. MD1 holds more dynamic info like nc programs. I imagine the main software is accesed through MD0. But I may be way off the mark here.


[1/17/2020 10:05 AM] .nikonian
I'll be putting up pics of the wire roughing and labels shortly


[1/17/2020 10:36 AM] fleetdog1
Md are mode pins, you pull them up or down to enter boot mode etc on startup, at least on the sh72a0

[1/29/2020 1:07 PM] pjlao307
Here's what I did on my Rav4 (not sure if this works on Honda since I can't test it):
```
cd /data/openpilot/selfdrive/boardd
pkill ./manager.py
./boardd
```
Then in another session/window:
```
/data/openpilot/selfdrive/car/fw_versions.py —scan
```

@mo-jord cd /data/openpilot/selfdrive/boardd

./boardd
Then in another session/window:

cd /data/openpilot/selfdrive/car/
Python fw_versions.py --scan
Start Car when message shows getting Vin

So, help me understand...  the rwd-X-ray script simply searches the Honda iHDS to find the proper rwd file to be used as a backup, correct?  I paid the $10 for the 1-day subscription a while back but couldn't manually find the rwd file for my vehicle. [2/8/2020 11:20 AM] vaggysag
Rwd x-ray is for extracting firmware from an rwd file


[2/8/2020 11:20 AM] vaggysag
Basically converts .rwd to .bin


[2/8/2020 11:21 AM] vaggysag
If your firmware isn't in i-hds you have to dump it from an EPS but that dump produces a .bin so you wouldn't even need rwd x-ray in that instance

[2/18/2020 2:51 PM] fleetdog1
if you have a oneplus3t, you can configure your eon to boot directly into autoecu using tristate switch by ```vim /data/data/com.termux/files/launch_openpilot.sh``` then replace the contents with ```cd /data/openpilot
switchstate=`cat /sys/devices/virtual/switch/tri-state-key/state`
if [ “$switchstate” = “3” ]; then
    git checkout autoecu
elif [ “$switchstate” = “2” ]; then
    git checkout joe1
fi
exec ./launch_openpilot.sh
   ```

[2/18/2020 3:46 PM] fleetdog1
just pushed an update to eps_tool: use ```python3 eps_tool.py -stock``` to make a stock rwd that supports modded "," fw. The fw in the rwd will be stock, but the rwd contains the modded version number in the header to allow it to be flashed onto modded fw.

**Enable OP longitudinal control to 0mph**.
 
Currently OP long does not work on either model due to the lack of a DSU. We understand the DSU function is integrated with Continental radar unit and can't simply be bypassed by unplugging.
LE/SE Camry models cut off stock ACC at 28mph
CH-R ACC is stop/go but stock only.
Continental Radar used in Camry LE/SE: 88210-06090
Continental Radar for CH-R: 88210-F4040

-Current community bounty (hardware OR software solution): $750
-Current comma.ai bounty (software only solution): $2000

[2/28/2020 11:49 PM] clarity.bru
I think it's important to surface the eps_modified value to the user before and/or during the drive.  If somebody has 2x torque and then doesn't the next time and doesn't realize it, they may end up in a ditch.  I encountered this issue (minus the ditch part).  My eps is modified and I was testing some code and it resulted in a check engine light that lingered for a day.  Whenever the check engine light was on, I only had 1x torque.


[2/29/2020 12:09 AM] stinkypenguin
@FleetDog what about mead? Come have a glass on me.


[2/29/2020 12:09 AM] stinkypenguin
Just made some lol


[2/29/2020 12:19 AM] chrismccammon
that doesn't make sense, 2x wouldn't just stop existing unless you reflashed to stock but then openpilot wouldn't tag it as modified anymore


[2/29/2020 12:33 AM] clarity.bru
In theory, yes.  This is a corner case.  Something happened that prevented OP from reading the EPS FW correctly.  I suspect that my check engine light has something to do with it.  From a safety perspective I think we should warn  the driver if it detected a change from 2x torque to 1x torque.


[2/29/2020 1:25 AM] chrismccammon
But if it goes back to 1x it's not like it would oversteer or anything, since it's just using the deadzone. Or am i missing something?


[2/29/2020 2:13 AM] clarity.bru
We are creatures of habit.  If somebody has gone through a sharp curve with the 2x torque modification 1000 times, they will assume that OP will be able to do it the next time.  What if, like me, on their next drive there is an check engine light during startup, where OP does not correctly read the FW and now only has 1x torque.  During this drive the car won't be able make the curve and might drift off of the road if the driver is not attentive enough.


[2/29/2020 2:13 AM] clarity.bru
If your car lost its ability to steer 200%, wouldn't you want to know before you began driving?


[2/29/2020 5:24 AM] fleetdog1
i think you should be paying enough attention to the road that you dont crash no matter what openpilot does if you are operating it within the safety model

{Reactions}
👍

[2/29/2020 9:07 AM] clarity.bru
This is a clear safety risk to me.  I think it's worth the effort to code a warning message.  It shouldn't be more than 100 lines of code.  This could save a live.  I will definitely code this for my branch.


[2/29/2020 9:34 AM] cfranyota
Nobody is forcing you to mod your EPS either. I think “Keep your Hands on the Wheel” is enough warning


[2/29/2020 9:34 AM] cfranyota

    self.eps_modified = False
    for fw in CP.carFw:
      if fw.ecu == "eps" and b"," in fw.fwVersion:
        print("EPS FW MODIFIED!")
        self.eps_modified = True
```

[2/29/2020 6:18 PM] fleetdog1

[2/29/2020 10:12 PM] audiojaguar
Joe the man with the answers


[2/29/2020 10:48 PM] fleetdog1
Just pushed an update to eps tool. Max torque was less than 2x before due to another clamp in the firmware. The top value in the table isn't actually the max torque because of the clamp, so we were far lower than 2x before. Input torque (after math) is clamped at 0x067F, but the index table goes to 0x6EE. So the value corresponding to 0x06EE is never reached. Before it was something like 1.5-1.7 depending on the car I think. I fixed all of them. CR-V was unaffected.

{Reactions}
👀 (6) 🙌🏻 (5)

[3/4/2020 8:10 AM] fleetdog1

[3/4/2020 10:41 AM] kiril_e

[3/4/2020 10:42 AM] fkndean_

[3/4/2020 10:43 AM] kiril_e

[3/4/2020 10:43 AM] kiril_e

Still working on trying to get torqueV vs angle error to work lol


[3/4/2020 10:46 AM] fkndean_
Type errors, should be able to fix soon


[3/4/2020 11:03 AM] fleetdog1
No, torqueV is the command sent via 0xe4. Before it was just a linear command to steer max 0xf00. Now since we have stretched the output response in the top end from a00, we also stretch openpilot so it knows that its not linear.

We tried adding breakpoints for each value in the torque table but that resulted in sloppier steering. So it seems that the curve in the fw is designed to provide better precision, and flattening that out has negative impacts. So we just have 2 stage interp, flat to a00 and then squeeze the top so it isnt treated like the same slope as the bottom.

{Reactions}
👍

[3/4/2020 11:07 AM] fkndean_
Yeah i remember when each value was in bp and v


[3/4/2020 11:13 AM] kiril_e
@FleetDog what is the range of actuators.steer and what does it physically correspond to?


[3/4/2020 11:16 AM] fleetdog1
Doesnt physically correspond to anything, the eps receives the command on 0xe4, does a bunch of math which is documented in my rwd xray repo thanks to Alex, and then puts it into that torque table as a value from 0 to 6ee (clamped at 67f prior to table) to obtain the output torque which then goes through a bunch more math and controls the eps motor itself. I can send you my research paper on the fw but I’m not sure if it should be public


[3/4/2020 11:18 AM] kiril_e
It's getting multiplied by the last value of the BP table so I assumed it was some sort of percentage of max torque


[3/4/2020 11:20 AM] kiril_e
Actuators.steer is an input to the apply steer function


[3/4/2020 11:50 AM] kiril_e
@FleetDog could you provide some more clarification


[3/4/2020 11:51 AM] fleetdog1
Hmm I am explaining it slightly incorrectly, @gregjhogan wrote that maybe he can clarify


[3/4/2020 11:52 AM] fleetdog1
My understanding is that the interp takes bp as input scale and v as output scale

[3/8/2020 12:44 PM] vaggysag
@Car Driver if your flash fails after the fw has been erased your EPS usually defaults to a different part number so you'd just make an rwd for that part number and flash it again. As for obtaining the bin, you can either dump or reuse if you somehow come across one


[3/8/2020 12:45 PM] lazarus_224

[3/8/2020 12:45 PM] vaggysag
No problem!


[3/8/2020 12:46 PM] vaggysag
Anyone who is having trouble tuning should use this script to see how their changes work
https://github.com/commaai/openpilot/blob/master/selfdrive/debug/internal/measure_steering_accuracy.py


[3/8/2020 12:46 PM] vaggysag
It has been suggested that you do at least a 10 min drive on a straight road for consistency and to make it statistically significant. If your test sample is too small it won't be as useful


[3/8/2020 3:20 PM] fleetdog1
The rwds made by my tool already contain the default revert version as a compatible version so they also function as recovery rwds for every car.

[3/24/2020 4:18 PM] jzluo.
@FleetDog


[3/24/2020 4:51 PM] fleetdog1
Interesting. Same route? @gregjhogan did you do this test with the sedan? I wonder if the range from a00 to f00 has higher rate limit from another table? I was thinking this might be the case


[3/24/2020 4:51 PM] fleetdog1
Looks like limiting the request range to a00 is not helping actually


[3/24/2020 4:58 PM] fleetdog1
In this case, maybe we should try interping it down so that a00 to f00 is squeezed into like a00 to b00


[3/24/2020 4:58 PM] fleetdog1
To take advantage of whatever is happening up there while the torque is flat


[3/24/2020 6:09 PM] jzluo.
yeah same route


[3/24/2020 9:29 PM] fleetdog1
I see the sample sizes now. I was looking at the higher angles. It seems like they are more than .03 apart but need more samples. I am suggesting that the a00 to f00 range may have other beneficial characteristics like a higher steer rate (for the lane departure wiggle) and maybe we can get the best of both worlds by squeezing that into a00 to b00 using interpolation to reduce the wind down delay without cutting that range off completely. I can take another look at the fw to see if another table has different values in that range that may be useful despite the torque plateau


[3/24/2020 9:44 PM] jzluo.
if it's still helpful I'll go on some longer drives


[3/24/2020 9:47 PM] fleetdog1
It would have to have more curvy sections, like greg said this shouldnt affect anything around zero


[3/24/2020 9:59 PM] disgracedpilot
Keep in mind that it takes 4x the samples to cut the standard deviation of a mean-estimate sampling in half. Can probably take that .03 variation number as something like a 95% = 2 sigma value for a mature sampling.


[3/24/2020 10:02 PM] disgracedpilot
If there's any need for slightly more detailed statistics in that script, maybe I can take a look at it and submit a pull request for it? Maybe a little confidence interval computation can help judge the maturity of results.

```python
eps_modified = False
for fw in car_fw:
  if fw.ecu == "eps" and b"," in fw.fwVersion:
    eps_modified = True
```

[5/22/2020 8:01 PM] korayz
cd /data/openpilot/selfdrive/boardd
pkill ./manager.py
./boardd
Then in another session/window:

cd /data/openpilot/selfdrive/car/
Python fw_versions.py --scan
Start Car when message shows getting Vin

[5/29/2020 7:49 PM] phil8702
I am a beginner too. I just did a search for RWD to find out what it was here on Discord. Found this and just entered it into the wiki. I am trying to get a complete list of every abbreviation used on this discord site. Some of these posts are incomprehensible. And  suggestions from some to just look it up underestimate the difficulty for a beginner to even know how to do that. For example, try looking up driver monitoring (DM). You will find DM used for Direct Messaging (should use PM for Private Message instead). You have pages and pages to go through. I am glad a wiki is being developed instead of impatiently blaming us brand new users. When every newcomer has a problem, the system is defective. So, the wiki really is a great attempt to solve this problem. WHENEVER you see a discord abbreviation which you do not understand, try to find the name and the meaning and then add it to the wiki in alphabetical order under  General Terms in the last section on discord terms and abbreviation. Use the format:

{Reactions}
👍 (2)

[6/12/2020 3:16 PM] stecky0784
> The K-Line is very different to a CAN Bus network and from most communication networks in general. A CAN Bus network, for example, does not have either a central or a primary ECM: all the ECMs are equal as they are all able to transmit messages along the network as well as receive messages.
> 
> On the K-Line network or any network compliant with ISO 9141, the direction of message flow is extremely important. The control of the network is dominated by the master ECM, and the message direction and timing depend on which ECM is talking (sending a message) and which ECMs are listening (waiting for a message). Two ECMs therefore cannot send a message at the same time, but have to wait in turn until allowed by the Master ECM.


[6/12/2020 3:17 PM] stecky0784
https://www.picoauto.com/library/automotive-guided-tests/k-line/#:~:text=The%20K%2DLine%20is%20very,most%20communication%20networks%20in%20general.&text=Note%202%3A%20A%20CAN%20Bus,intended%20to%20support%20diagnostic%20equipment.

{Embed}
https://www.picoauto.com/library/automotive-guided-tests/k-line/
Network - K-Line
K-line of ISO9141-2 & keyword 2000485A

[6/22/2020 10:22 AM] jsbour9630
If it's like mine, the coating looks to be silicone or something like it.  Two approaches:  1. Lots of light, magnification, and just the right angle, and you might be able to just read the P/N through the coating.  My guess:  PD70F4154GCA2 or PD70F4155GCA2, this based simply on knowing it's a V850-something, what I can see of the part number, and the electrical connections around the chip (crystal connections, power connections, etc).  2: Scrape the silicone (or whatever it is) off, then look; if nothing after that, try rubbing the top of the chip with white silicone grease or maybe chalk dust so that the grease goes into the etched lettering on top of the chip.  Of course, do all this carefully, and no guarantees...good luck!


[6/22/2020 10:23 AM] stecky0784
good tips... thanks


[6/22/2020 10:33 AM] jsbour9630
To further clarify:  I used my fingernail to scrape the coating off; I wouldn't use anything harder than that.  Once the coating comes off, it'll probably take any paint/dye off that was making the part number visible, but in all cases I've seen so far (though mine's an SH72A0, not a V850 so YMMV) the part number's engraved, so you can "re-paint" it by rubbing on something white.  Again, good magnification, lighting, and angle are all important.  It's amazing how much angle matters, and sometimes you have to look at the chip almost edge-on to see the lettering.


[6/22/2020 10:47 PM] ngianoplus
Wishing you the best of luck @bit-chemist , watching closely!


[6/22/2020 11:00 PM] bitchemist3476
I encountered this


[6/22/2020 11:07 PM] bitchemist3476
That layer does act like cured silicone.


[6/22/2020 11:09 PM] bitchemist3476
I removed the layer with some q-tips and acetone, but I used a lot of q-tips and the minimum amount of acetone, and only on top of the chips to remove the layer, not on the PCB


[6/22/2020 11:13 PM] bitchemist3476
Once I had dissolved the layer I rinsed the chips and surrounding PCB with isopropyl alcohol.


[6/22/2020 11:14 PM] bitchemist3476
Chip numbers incoming

[6/30/2020 10:01 AM] hondacivicwyde
I bet the FW is cross compatible with my Civic Sedan


[6/30/2020 10:01 AM] hondacivicwyde
@bit-chemist


[6/30/2020 2:43 PM] bitchemist3476
Possibly. As as car lines begin to use global platforms within their companies (or groups) with common parts, hardware will start to be common. Is there an electrical / mechanical difference between any of the EPS motors amongst all US 2020 Civic Hatchback trim levels? I haven't checked, but I suspect there is no difference. The only physical difference between trim levels with regard to the complete steering system is a different steering ratio. Thus, when the EPS for my 2020CivHatchSportTouring also fits the 2020CivHatchSport, and these are the trims with identical ratios, it's my hunch that if I successfully dump this fw, it will work for all 2017-2020 Civic Hatch Sport and Sport Touring CVTs. That said...


[6/30/2020 2:48 PM] bitchemist3476
This firmware is a collection of code and data. By updating the torque table, we're updating the data. What if the steering ratio is just a variable that we can update? If that were confirmed, I would begin to wonder about a base firmware with only different data like you mention

Is there a way for the C2/EON to flash firmwares automatically or does it have to be done separately?


[7/23/2020 6:48 PM] csouers
if you mean car firmware, then no


[7/23/2020 6:48 PM] csouers
you could write it you really wanted to. it's all open source


[7/23/2020 6:58 PM] doktor_sleepless
You really don't want your c2/eon to be flashing your eps automatically in the same way eons automatically flash pandas. You should be the one to intiate it, you should be hyper aware when it's happening, and should closely be monitoring it until it finishes flashing.   That being said, you can use a c2/eon to flash your car instead of a laptop if that's all you want.  Modying the firmware file is still needs to be done seperately on a regular computer though. Perhaps you meant having the eon/c2  modfy the firmware automatically before flashing.  I guess anything is possible, but not currently.

[8/15/2020 7:02 AM] bitchemist3476
You have a bunch of different computers on the car. One is the steering motor computer, that fw mods for steering torque. Ktuner etc talk to a different computer, the power train or engine control module. They change the air/fuel and timing.


[8/15/2020 7:03 AM] bitchemist3476
Two different computers, different fw, won't overwrite.

[8/31/2020 8:42 AM] bitchemist3476
@doktor careful with the spreadsheet data collapse. I made most of the additional columns while looking up my own EPS, to try and bring clarity to the issue. I broke out individual years because people were asking searchable questions if they would just look. Also, Civic hatchback sports and sport touring trims are now wrong for model years. The sport sensing is only 2019-2020. Sport Touring is 2017-2020. This is cross referenced by hardware part number fitment on a number of parts websites. 

I'm certainly not against making data more accessible and easier to use. It's also not my data or spreadsheet. I encourage improving usability.


[8/31/2020 11:43 AM] rapsin4

[9/23/2020 6:38 AM] doktor_sleepless
Your 39990-TGL-E130 firmware is a new one here.  It's not the same firmware used on the American hatchback, so it's not compatible with the current mod. If you really want to mod your car,  the first step would be to buy the exact eps unit from your car, and extract the firmware.   Here's a guide from joe1. https://discord.com/channels/469524606043160576/469532500335656972/638214668124553267


[9/23/2020 6:48 AM] chandrakanth2512

It's a lot less intimidating than it looks. You don't even necessary have to solder.

[9/24/2020 8:19 AM] doktor_sleepless
Also, you may not necessarily need to buy the eps from the exact same trim as your car.  The pattern so far has been that all trims within the same civic model share the same firmware.  You can widen your search for used parts that way. Look for any eps from 17-18 hatchbacks.  Sport versions of civics should be considered their own unique model though.  I'm not sure what the euro equivalent of sport is called.


[9/24/2020 8:20 AM] doktor_sleepless
I can't guarantee it, but if you find a cheap used eps that doesn't match your trim exactly, it may be worth a shot.

I'm sure it's possible to use a panda for performance mods. Nothing is preventing you or anyone else from starting a competing engine tune business.  If it was a viable business venture, someone would have probably done it already.

[12/26/2020 3:22 PM] collink80
Seems like you're intentionally trolling but I was being serious. How about I phrase it another way: if I installed a 110 outlet in my house but wired it 480V single phase and you then plugged your laptop into it and the laptop blew up, whose fault is that? You plugged in seeing that it's a 110V outlet. I violated your assumption by putting 4x the voltage on it presumably for some special project I was doing. I really don't think it would be your fault if you assumed that a 110V outlet had 110V running to it. Likewise, if you see a USB port on a device that you would reasonably assume you could connect to then  it's logical to plug into it. We're not talking about some flash drive you found in the street - that would make your analogy apt. Instead this is a device that someone owned and wanted to connect to. They messed up how to do that but I really would not consider it their fault that someone misappropriated the USB plug for a non-standard purpose.

[12/26/2020 6:50 PM] doktor_sleepless
He had to deliberately pop open a  rearview mirror cover and specifically plug in a USB A to USB C cable to a random mystery box.  I think 99.99%  of people in the world would be safe from ever accidentally recreating this unfortunate event. Someone attempting a torque mod without knowing what hardware is what would be that .01%.  I believe there was a similar danger to plugging in a laptop to the fake ethernet port in the old giraffe, but again it's one of those things that there's almost no reason to ever try.   There's  still some danger with phones and hopefully the new hardware takes that into account, but at least  it seems phones nowadays should be mostly safe.

I think the problem here is that he seems to have attempted a torque mod going in blind.  That  may have it's own set of problems bigger than a laptop frying.


[12/28/2020 12:00 PM] divemebreh8292
Does the Eon run on 12v?


[12/28/2020 3:16 PM] _livid.
This should not be the solution. The whole point of the usb c spec is that it's not relying on protection. It's absolutely unacceptable for them to use the usb c port and not conform to the most basic of standards which is "don't supply power over the vbus pins until requested"


[12/28/2020 3:20 PM] _livid.
It's not a case of "it makes sense" to not plug random usb cables into your shit it's a case of following the goddamn specs for the connector you're using. They didn't need to use Rj45 for the giraffe, there are other solutions, fuck there are keyed rj45s which would stop you plugging it into your laptop. This violation of standards will not fly when comma create hardware for consumers and it's utterly irresponsible to be pushing it on carmakers. No carmaker is going to put obd-c on their cars until it's redone so that it won't have a chance of exploding people's phones


[12/28/2020 4:38 PM] divemebreh8292
Power negotiation would have saved my laptop, and it would be _really_ nice to be able to view live data by plugging directly into the USB C port on the harness itself. I understand that Comma doesn't state anything anywhere that would lead someone to believe the device is capable of this.

The no walkthroughs rule in this channel is completely understandable. That, along side the message in onboarding asking people to take the time to use the search and the link to "how to ask questions the smart way" lead me to believe that I would have had an easier time and received better answers by giving things a go on my own first. I always got the sense that the hacker community was quicker and happier to help those who showed initiative, and the comma servers seem to embody the hacker spirit, hence my "going in blind" first.

I would like to think I didn't come off as malicious or dumb as the whole "stick in the spokes" or the "raw dog hookers" analogy; I didn't know that plugging into the port would send 12v back up through the line, and that isn't something that would be obvious by reading any of the chat history or the USB C specifications. I'm certainly comfortable admitting I made the mistake of trusting without verifying.

At this point, it isn't really an issue. I've managed to get the help I needed from a few extremely helpful individuals, I found the parts I needed to repair my laptop, and it's not like I'm hounding Comma trying to claim it's their fault. I do think adam, Livid, collink80 and others have a point about sticking to established standards. At any rate, I have a saying for these sort of situations, "this is how we learn things" 😁

[1/25/2021 9:41 PM] steveham
Since CAN is differential, I'm pretty sure for the Odyssey it's 6 and 7.  Looking at your Civic picture you see that your CAN pins of 4 and 6 have their traces adjacent to each other.  This is only true for pins 6 and 7 with the Odyssey.

So 6 is CAN_H and 7 is CAN_L.  8 would also be my guess for ignition based on your Civic photo.


[1/25/2021 9:43 PM] steveham
One way to confirm with ignition is to follow the trace on your Civic EPS to see where it goes and compare against the Odyssey board.


[1/25/2021 10:01 PM] doktor_sleepless

That's why said maybe the ignition is 12 because it goes to a green thing, but so does 11 and 13. I had forgotten the odyssey board was kind of different when I made the picture.

We're so close - we think we have the torque tables figured out, but without testing on the bench, it's too risky to flash to a car.

[3/18/2021 1:53 PM] georgehotz (pinned)
a heads up, if we find you using a torque mod with substantially over a 2x multiple on steering torque, you and your device will be banned for life from the comma ecosystem. this goes into effect on monday (3/22/21)

aside from completely removing your device from touching comma's servers, you will not be able to hide from this. the detector will detect the real torque being applied to the wheel (we can do this from only the video). so if you are running substantially more than a 2x torque mod, this is your one warning to lower it.

{Reactions}
👎 (4) 💩 (4)

[3/18/2021 2:00 PM] jlapin.
Whoever is maintaining the google sheets doc, I submitted via the google form but it looks like the 2017 CR-V might have different EPS firmware based on the trim. The doc has 39990-TLA-A030 but it looks like the EX/XL-L is actually 39990-TLA-A040.


[3/18/2021 2:01 PM] georgehotz
Pinned a message.


[3/18/2021 2:04 PM] georgehotz
some of the torque mods i have seen are not safe, and consistent with comma's policy, if we find you doing things we consider unsafe, we can't stop you, we just want nothing to do with you.


[3/18/2021 2:07 PM] doktor_sleepless
Will it always be 2x for the foreseeable future? Can this policy be changed with max torque injection tests using slightly higher multipliers, or is that not enough to prove safety?


[3/18/2021 2:09 PM] georgehotz
not necessarily, and injection tests should be good enough. if you do injection testing at 2.3x and show it's reasonable, i think that's okay. the bans won't be issued to the devices that are on the edge of reasonable torque. but i've seen some people jacking it up to 4x+ without a concern for safety, and these users will be banned.


[3/18/2021 2:13 PM] georgehotz
this isn't like park assist, we believe there are safe ways to do torque mods. but there's also unsafe ways to do them, and this is intended to push against that. the most egregious offenders will be banned first. we aren't saying 2x is even safe, 2x may be dangerous in practice as well. comma doesn't endorse any torque mods, and everyone doing these mods should be doing their own injection testing.


[3/18/2021 2:15 PM] georgehotz
the devices (and users) that will be banned are the ones that kept raising the limit because they wanted it to be able to take a sharp turn without any care for the behavior of the system in other conditions. remember that that same torque used on that turn can also be applied randomly at any point on the highway. the model is QM

{Reactions}
👎 👍

[3/18/2021 2:35 PM] jlapin.
Is there a potential in the future for max torque to change dynamically with speed?


[3/18/2021 3:20 PM] georgehotz
it's possible. if you show you are putting real effort into the safety and have thought through the implications before driving around with your mods, this isn't who i mean to target. there's certainly ways to do torque mods safely, and i don't mean to discourage safe research. i do mean to discourage people who think more torque is always better and just set the multiplier big because it's an option

{Reactions}
👍 (4)

[3/18/2021 4:26 PM] georgehotz
if your torque mod is well calibrated to be able to drive on the highway at a reasonable speed, without much extra torque than that, this isn't what i'm talking about, even if the actual multiplier is slightly higher than 2. all cars are different. i'm talking about people trying to make 90 degree turns at speed with their torque mods. if it can follow a 90 degree turning road, it can also follow that same "road" even if the real road is perfectly straight. openpilot is a level 2 system, it is not designed to work on every road.


[3/18/2021 4:34 PM] georgehotz
@Martin do you have an issue with this policy? if you have safety testing to justify otherwise, please post it. but i recall the model failure you had, and the solution isn't to fix the model. while we can improve the model, it is and always will be QM, meaning the rest of the system has to enforce the safety. openpilot's functional safety cannot rely on the model ever doing anything sane.


[3/18/2021 4:34 PM] edwardapollo
Aren't fw mods done in that the torque is dependent on the velocity of the car as it is?


[3/18/2021 4:35 PM] georgehotz
our safety model is here: https://github.com/commaai/openpilot/blob/master/SAFETY.md

having torque mods that are too high violate part 2 "The vehicle must not alter its trajectory too quickly for the driver to safely react. This means that while the system is engaged, the actuators are constrained to operate within reasonable limits."


[3/18/2021 4:36 PM] georgehotz
@EdwardApollo yes, but not enough. if you set that multiplier too high, it can be very dangerous


[3/18/2021 4:36 PM] edwardapollo

[3/29/2021 8:25 PM] steveham
I got the FW script to work by turning on my car's auxilary power so everything is powered on but the engine isn't running, running the script, then starting my car's engine when it says getting Vin


Full steps:

In 1st Putty window:
cd /data/openpilot/selfdrive/boardd
pkill ./manager.py
./boardd

Turn on your car's auxilary power so it's powered on but the engine isn't running

In 2nd Putty window:
cd /data/openpilot/selfdrive/car
python fw_versions.py --scan

Start your car's engine when message shows getting Vin

[4/19/2021 11:44 PM] doktor_sleepless
Don't really need a hacksaw to take off the adhesive.  Just pried it off with a flat head screwdriver and a hammer.
That's the only guide, but the readme from the repo I linked also has more or less the same instructions.  Just install the prerequisites and run `python sh-2a.py`.  That pdf says bootmode.py, but that's just an older version of the script that has since been renamed.


[4/19/2021 11:55 PM] doktor_sleepless
Also make sure to trace the pins.  The location of the pads (vias?) are probably in  slightly different locations than what's pictured in that pdf.  The odyssey pcb was different from the civic/crv pcb.

[5/12/2021 5:08 PM] reddn

I think your eps probably has a v850e2 processor

[5/14/2021 7:45 PM] doktor_sleepless
The first time I did it with the needles it took me a few days of fiddling.  What ultimately worked was using pliers to push the needles in hard into the vias snuggly.  But I wouldn't recommend it because the second time I did this on another eps, I snapped the needle inside, and it was a pain to take out.  Others have had better luck with needles though.

[5/14/2021 7:46 PM] doktor_sleepless

[9/11/2021 3:13 PM] doktor_sleepless
There's only a couple civic sedan eps firmwares for 2016-2018 as far as we know.   39990-TBA-A030 for the ones made in North America. And 39990-TEG-A010 for the ones made in Japan. If your vin starts with a J, you have the Japanese civic.

[9/13/2021 5:51 PM] doktor_sleepless

[9/13/2021 5:53 PM] doktor_sleepless
They do want it to turn past 25 degrees.  They care more about rate limits than the actual max angle as far as safety goes.

[9/26/2021 3:54 PM] darthus
Hey everyone, I'm running a 2x Torque mod on my Honda Civic 2016 Touring (which to my understanding has a default tuning and detection in the core OP code for 2X). I had heard that a reasonable torque mod higher than that (ie 2.3 or 2.5) still won't be banned for old Hondas. anyone have a recommendation for them that works well? 2X can take most turns on the freeway, but like going through a freeway change, even if I manually change down to the recommended speed limit will often feel like it's drifting out of the lane.


[9/28/2021 3:43 PM] energee_
Raising these higher than the values in carstate eps_modified won't actually give you more torque, it will effectively just raise the steering rate at which the wheel turns to the requested value. Also, you would have to maintain proportional and integral values appropriate for these changes.

This is exactly what a few people have done with Mazdas. Pre-2019 cars have low toque, but 2019+ cx9's have double the torque, and can be swapped into many 2014+ Mazda models. Doesn't even have to be the same model either, we had someone swap a cx9 EPS into a cx5, and I personally have a 2018 eps in my 2014, but that was just to add LKAS.

[1/30/2022 5:36 PM] firstsrif

[1/30/2022 8:10 PM] steveham
The firmware from an existing Civic 22 EPS module needs to be extracted first. Nothing can be done until that happens, but even if it was it’s not a guarantee it can be reverse engineered.

I’m guessing it will take many months for a used 2022 EPS module to show up on a junk parts website for someone to even attempt it. It needs to be cut open to access the inside to extract the firmware.

The alternative is if the Civic’s EPS firmware version matches a previously modded version. If that’s the case then it should be doable, but the likelihood of that is almost zero.

{Reactions}
💯

[2/24/2022 1:51 AM] mmmorks
you have to switch to a proprietary UDS mode 0x60 to access it


[2/24/2022 1:53 AM] mmmorks
the key algorithm for mode 60 level 3 access is the simplest one too 😝


[2/24/2022 3:36 AM] mmmorks
interestingly the pilot firmware doesn't implement write memory by identifier or request download so autoecu won't work as is

{Reactions}
😢

[2/25/2022 2:01 AM] mmmorks
You’re correct that there are multiple implementations of UDS in the Honda Pilot firmware. I’ve found one in the boot loader too now, but it doesn’t have any of the standard self-flashing SIDs either which is odd. I’m figuring this out by inspection of the firmware rather than testing. There are other UDS functions to write memory implemented and I’m still working through the firmware to find all the details.


[2/25/2022 2:24 AM] mmmorks
hah, and as I type that I just found another implementation of UDS in the boot loader that implements request download, transfer, etc. 🙂 Just need to figure out how to use this one and how the checksumming for this firmware works and I should be able to reflash too

{Reactions}
👍 (2)

[2/27/2022 10:29 PM] mmmorks
urgh... tearing my hair out trying to figure out how the checksumming works on the v850 honda EPS. I know where they are in the image, but I can't find code that does anything resembling a checksum. There's one place that calculates the total, but only to verify that it's non-zero. I thought maybe it was using the built-in CRC peripheral, but nope. Clearly honda cares about them because they are different between different versions of the firmware. They are 4 bytes, but the negative sum and sum of bytes method doesn't find matches that line up. The matches that are found are false positives. There's even a CRC lookup table that doesn't have any references to it in the code.


[2/28/2022 1:56 AM] mmmorks
is that the only time? It isn't checked on boot too?

[4/7/2022 3:46 AM] doktor_sleepless
That's at standstill though. Back when I experimented with higher torque mods, I could never get the the steering wheel to turn more than  45 degrees at the minimum set speed of 25mph.  Even using a 6x multiplier wouldn't do it.  Like maybe past 3x, it stopped making a noticeable difference because of that 45 degree bottleneck.  I've been told this is also the experience of people with  different Honda models with a  torque mod.
Below 25mph, I think the angle limit goes away though. When I installed a comma pedal on my nidec civic, which removed the minimum set speed, I found I could get close to 90 degrees at 20mph just with 2x.  (on a curve with a large radius at least)
It's basically why I gave up on higher multipliers and just stuck with 2x since higher multipliers don't make a huge difference for my everyday driving.  I'm sure  it would make a difference for turns at intersections at lower speeds, but I don't care too much about that since I don't think openpilot is reliably capable of doing that yet.  I would love to do some legit  injection testing with a higher multiplier and maybe try to get it merged, but the 45 degree limit at higher speeds is depressing.

[3/20/2023 10:45 AM] mcall
This is a pain to get working, you can just install brave instead using these commands:

$ echo "deb [arch=arm64] https://brave-browser-apt-release.s3.brave.com/ stable main" | sudo tee /etc/apt/sources.list.d/brave-browser-release.list

$ sudo apt update --allow-insecure-repositories

$ sudo apt -y --allow-unauthenticated install brave-browser

[4/24/2023 1:08 PM] mrnerdhair
I'm trying to reverse-engineer the Clarity firmware patches to verify their safety. Does anyone happen to know why the torque calculator excel sheet gives different table values for a 2x multiplier than the ones actually used in the 2x firmware patches? (In the patches, the last field of lines 3-6 is 0x2d00 vs 0x2400 from the sheet, and the last 3 fields of line 7 are 0x16c4/0x1852/0x2f16 vs 0x119a/0x1ae7/0x23b4.)


[4/24/2023 1:09 PM] mrnerdhair
And does anyone happen to know why there are 7 different rows, and what (if anything?) in the car ever actually changes the STEER_CONFIG_INDEX?


[4/24/2023 1:10 PM] mrnerdhair
(Also what do the filter_table and new_table mods do? I haven't found any docs on those.)


[4/25/2023 8:21 AM] vaggysag
As far as we know steer config index never changes (that we have seen). Stock seems to only use the first 5 rows so usually we use the last 2 rows to expand the range. The filter table has to be tuned with the torque table otherwise your EPS motor will sound like an impact wrench


[4/25/2023 8:24 AM] vaggysag
Though if you eventually want to use the torque steering controller you should modify the torque table to be linear


[4/25/2023 3:14 PM] mrnerdhair
I thought STEER_CONFIG_INDEX was the row index?


[5/2/2023 9:21 AM] vaggysag
Sorry I wrote that right after waking up--what I meant was stock seems to use the first 6 values of the row and we expand the range with the last 3 values of the row. From what we have seen steer config index always stays at one value and that value determines which row gets used (There are usually 7 rows but only 1 ever gets used).

[4/4/2024 11:27 PM] mmmorks
should be similar/same


[4/4/2024 11:32 PM] mmmorks
0x18da30f1 is the CAN address though. The start/end addresses for the tool are the ROM start and end addresses. Typically the boot loader is the first few thousand bytes followed by the main application code as well as data sections


[4/4/2024 11:33 PM] mmmorks
the CAN address in the tool is just the XX values in 0x18daXXf1

@jrdsgl I'm on 2.5x. at all speeds it feels safe, however, I've noticed that at least with SunnyPilot, which allows "always on" lane alignment, I have an issue where in either weird situations (typically gas station parking lots) or (today) a point where I was trying to get over, and the diagonally-back-from me driver shorted me on room, requiring a rather oddly angled squeeze, there tends to be an issue, where if I'm deviating hard enough from the aligned path, and have to fight(it's pretty subtle, so "fight" is a bit harsh) the wheel, LKAS cuts out entirely requiring a restart. I'm not 100% sure this is a torque-mod issue, but in the week before I modded, I did not encounter it. The manual solution is to remember to disable LKAS right before making awkward or comma-confused maneuvers, though there may be an automated solution I haven't yet pursued.


[4/29/2025 8:43 PM] malsententia
like I think it could be mitigated if the software could be like "okay you're really ignoring what I think is right, I give up for a few seconds". But it's possibly firmware related as in, with normal firmware, it can't resist the driver's wheel use to the same degree, and it cuts out because it feels more deliberately "fought"
Regardless that isn't entirely safety matter....but it does kill all smart features including the auto braking, till 20-30s of car-off, so sort of a safety issue. But still mitigatable.

[4/29/2025 11:39 PM] vaggysag
Steering to absolute 0 causes a few issues so we picked a value just above 0 for most mods. If you're ever curious you can find the address in the eps_tool file then open your modded firmware in ghidra and find what it is set to currently

[5/21/2025 2:49 PM] mary_online
2024 Honda CR-V Hybrid (Bosch C) – EPS Torque Mod Info

Looking to raise steering torque limits on Bosch C EPS. Here's what I’ve found:

EPS is locked down — UDS over CAN-FD + seed/key security.

No public tool supports Bosch C EPS flashing yet.

You’d need:

CAN-FD sniffer (ValueCAN 4-2, CANable FD)

Bench harness for EPS

Bosch-compatible flasher (KESSv3, Autel, etc.)

UDS tool + key algorithm (not public)


Even with firmware dump, you'd have to reverse torque tables, patch, checksum, and reflash.


High brick risk + no known success cases publicly.

[5/22/2025 1:30 PM] jrdsgl
what model civic are you running with those results? sounds unsafe?


[5/22/2025 2:12 PM] brettpakkala
2018 Civic Sedan Touring. Nidec.


[5/22/2025 2:12 PM] brettpakkala
It’s not unsafe per-se because the only way I’m able to achieve such degrees is by driving at under 5mph


[5/22/2025 2:14 PM] brettpakkala
I’ve made my own files and played with the tables quite a bit, but even with a higher number it gets to the point of diminishing returns. It isn’t like an “oh I’ll set the tables at 8x and see what happens”. Above 2x the improvements are only apparent below 25mph, above 3x the improvements are only apparent below 12mph. So what this is really doing helps mostly with stuff like turns at intersections and such, everywhere else is pretty limited. And the thing as a whole is.

Oh AND WARNING!!!

Do NOT go above around 4-5x because it is dangerous. Not because the torque is too much, but because the car will start to steer in the INCORRECT direction when torque commands get sent. So basically it’s unusable. No point on going higher.


[5/22/2025 2:20 PM] brettpakkala
I also run my own custom branch which has something that helps out the smoothness a ton with the torque controller. **Reduced friction on turns.**

Friction is basically error correction and too much makes the wheel rapidly oscillate. It benefits the stability of the controls greatly when driving down long straight roads due to some level of slop in the steering mechanism, but obviously you don’t have any of that slop if you’re already turned in a single direction. So by reducing friction on instances of lateral acceleration reaching higher amounts, you effectively get the best of both worlds. FSD-13-like turns with rock-solid straights.


[5/28/2025 6:29 PM] jrdsgl
Interesting, thanks for sharing. Any video examples of it in action?

[8/17/2025 1:57 PM] the_roen
I think it’s a bit short sighted without understanding the specifics, given that the practical maximum of modified applied torque on a Honda EPS rack is less than the stock torque of other brands’ EPS rack which OP currently supports in upstream

@adeeb 

I could max out every value possible on the Honda rack and I still couldn’t generate as much torque as a stock Toyota, Hyundai or Tesla rack.


[8/17/2025 1:59 PM] adeebshihadeh
we're not gonna ban forks for it or anything, but we're just choosing to no longer support it upstream

{Reactions}
👎 (3)

[8/17/2025 2:00 PM] the_roen

[8/17/2025 2:00 PM] adeebshihadeh

[8/17/2025 2:01 PM] adeebshihadeh

[8/17/2025 4:14 PM] jyoung8607

[8/17/2025 4:15 PM] the_roen
right now PID with torque mod and low-pass filter to rid the PID shakes is still top performer


[8/17/2025 4:15 PM] the_roen
torque controller with torque mod, NNFF-Lite and non-linear torque fix commit performs about 90% as well


[8/17/2025 4:16 PM] the_roen
torque controller with torque mod, NNFF and non-linear torque fix (if it's even called with NNFF active) performs the worst


[8/17/2025 4:18 PM] the_roen
didn't test torque controller with no NNFF options enabled


[8/17/2025 4:20 PM] jyoung8607
the lateral accel torque controller basically *is* the feedforward function, not sure what NNFF is doing on top


[8/17/2025 4:31 PM] the_roen
NNFF requires torque controller to be active, otherwise it's not doing anything


[8/17/2025 4:31 PM] the_roen
https://github.com/twilsonco/openpilot/blob/log-info/sec/1%20History%20and%20future%20of%20data-driven%20controls%20improvments.md


[8/18/2025 1:34 AM] twilsonco
NNFF is similar to the stock torque controller. Just replace the `torque_from_lateral_accel` function with the NN. Like how comma does with the Bolt with their own NN, but mine additionally uses lateral jerk and past/future lateral accel and road roll as inputs, and doesn't use aEgo.
